Preface |

This Report is prepared for submission to the Governor of the State of
Chhattisgarh under Article 151 of the Constitution of India.

The audit of expenditure by the Departments of the State Government
is conducted under Section 13 of the Comptroller and Auditor
General’s (Duties, Powers and Conditions of service) Act, 1971.

This Report presents the results of audit of expenditure of the
Government of Chhattisgarh.

The cases mentioned in this Report are those, which came to notice in
the course of test audit during the year 2012-13 as well as those, which
came to notice in earlier years, but could not be dealt with in the
previous Reports; matters relating to the period subsequent to 2012-13
have also been included, wherever necessary.

The audit has been conducted in conformity with the Auditing
Standards issued by the Comptroller and Auditor General of India.

vii


OVERVIEW

This Report comprises of three Chapters: the first Chapter contains the
financial profile of the State, planning and conduct of audit and follow-up on
Audit Reports, Chapter 2 of this Report deals with the findings of tive
Performance Audit reviews and four long paragraphs and Chapter 3 deals with
compliance audit in the various departments. The audit findings included in
Performance Audits and Compliance audit paragraphs in this report have total
money value of ¥ 460.99 crore.

The audit has been conducted in accordance with the Auditing Standards
prescribed for the Indian Audit and Accounts Department, Audit samples have
been drawn based on statistical sampling as well as risk based judgemental
sampling. The specific audit methodology adopted has been mentioned in each
Performance Audit. The audit conclusions have been drawn and
recommendations have been made taking into consideration the views of the
Government. A summary of main audit findings is presented in this overview.

(i)

Rashtriya Krishi Vikas Yojana (RKVY) was launched to incentivise states to
draw up plans for their agriculture sector more comprehensively so as to
achieve four per cent annual growth rate in agricultural sector during the
XI" Five year Plan (2007-2012) by ensuring a holistic development of
Agriculture and allied sectors. Although the State was able to achieve more
than six per cent annual growth rate in the Agriculture sector during the
period, there was delay in preparation and submission of District Agriculture
Plans and State Agriculture Plan. Lack of planning in implementation of the
projects was observed as many projects were not included in the State
Agriculture Plan. There was delay in release of funds to implementing
agencies and funds were not utilised efficiently resulting in non-release of
subsequent funds from Government of India. There were instances of
non-completion of construction works and under-utilisation of created
infrastructure. Monitoring of the scheme activities was ineffective, frequency
of State Level Sanctioning Committee meetings was very less and committee
required to be constituted as per Scheme guidelines to review the
implementation of RK VY was not constituted.

(Paragraph 2.1)

The Performance Audit of Indira Aways Yojana (IAY) was carried out to
examine the implementation of the scheme in the backdrop of its objectives to
provide housing for rural BPL homeless population and facilitate them to have
a complete functional dwelling unit with basic needs. Although the targets of
TAY during the period of Performance audit was almost fully achieved by the


Audit Report on General, Social and Economic (Non-PSUs) Sectors for the year ended 31 March 2013

Department, there was lack of monitoring at the Janpad Panchayat level in
the selection of beneficiaries by the Gram Sabhas leading to improper
preparation of TAY waitlist. The [AY funds were not kept in separate bank
accounts in JPs and the interest accumulated on TAY funds in Zilla
Panchayats was not being utilised. There was delay in release of State share
and also there was instances of inadmissible expenditure and diversion of
funds. There was lack of convergence of IAY with other schemes. The
monitoring and evaluation needed further strengthening.

(Paragraph 2.2)

(iii) | Review of the creation of infrastructure for National e-Governance

Plan and delivery of services to common citizens through Common
Service Centers.

The National e-Governance Plan (NeGP) envisaged three pillar model for
delivery of “web-enabled anytime, anywhere access” information and services
in rural areas. Government of Chhattisgarh (GoCG) had identified 10 key
services for providing Government to Citizen (G2C) services. The
Performance Audit of NeGP was carried out to examine the creation of
Information Technology (IT) and services delivery infrastructure by the State
which is essential for effective and cost efficient functioning of e-Governance.
Audit observed that establishment of pre-requisites like IT core infrastructure
temained unaccomplished and the project could not be made operational.
State Wide Area Network could not be utilised optimally in the absence of
availability of G2C Services and incomplete rollout of Common Service
Centers (CSCs) in rural areas. Interconnectivity between various departments
for information sharing and service delivery also could not be achieved. There
was lack of monitoring by High Level Committee. Overall, the objectives
envisaged in the NeGP guidelines could not be achieved

(Paragraph 2.3)

Development of ro:

Minimum Needs Programme (MNP)

The Performance Audit of Central Road Fund (CRF) and Minimum Needs
Programme (MNP) was carried out to examine whether selection of roads,
planning, fund management and execution of projects was carried out
economically, efficiently and effectively. Audit observed that due to
inadequate planning and non prioritisation of road, there was overlapping of
sanctions. The Public Works Department (PWD) failed to make detailed
survey and investigation before execution of road works which led to
significant variations in quantities and delay in completion of the works.
Execution of road works without availability of required land and forest
clearance delayed the works and increased the cost of the works. Failure of the
PWD to adhere to the Government’s instructions and contractual conditions
led to unauthorised diversion of MNP funds, non recovery of penalty, payment
of inadmissible price escalation, irregular execution of works, superfluous
expenditure, excess payment etc.

(Paragraph 2.4)



(v) Functioning of Sports and Youth Welfare Department.

The Sports and Youth Welfare Department is responsible for promoting and
conducting sports activities in the State. Audit of the Department was
conducted to examine the economy, efficiency and effectiveness of significant
activities. Audit observed deficiencies in planning such as non adherence to
Sports Policy 2001, preparation of incomplete Detailed Project Report (DPR)
for implementation of Panchayat Yuva Krida aur Khel Abhiyan (PYKKA) etc.
There were irregularities such as non-utilisation of funds, irregular purchases,
non-appointment of required number of Kridashree, shortfall in coverage of
village and block panchayat etc., in implementation of PYKKA. For hosting
the 37" National Games in 2017, no concrete steps had been initiated by the
State Government for creation of necessary infrastructure during the last three
and half years. There were irregularities such as excess expenditure on
escalation due to change in design and scope of work, undue aid to contractor
on mobilisation advance, etc. Shortage of manpower at various levels resulted
in ineffective implementation and monitoring of the activities.

(Paragraph 2.5)

Upgradation of Industrial Training Institutes through Public

Private Partnership

Technical Education contributes substantially to the socio-economic
development of the State and the Country as a whole. With the objective to
augment the skilled work force through upgradation of existing trade and
commencement of new trades in Industrial Training Institutes (ITIs) under
Public-Private Partnership mode, Government of India decided (October
2007) to upgrade the ITI into Centers for Excellence. Out of 118 ITIs in
Chhattisgarh, 42 ITIs were selected for upgradation. Audit observed that the
infrastructure required for upgradation and commencement of the new trade
were not developed and affiliation could not be obtained. There were instances
of blockage of funds, shortfalls in infrastructure and absence of regular
faculties. No steps were taken by the IMCs for revenue generation. The
internal control was weak, monitoring was improper and there was absence of
regular meeting by State Implementation Cell.

(Paragraph 2.6)

(vii) Provision of minimum basic infrastructure and other facilities at

Pre-Matric Tribal Hostels and Ashrams

With an aim to bring the Scheduled Tribes and Schedule Caste children to
schools from remote areas and to continue their studies, State Government
formulated the Hostel and Ashram Rules 2010. Audit reviewed the
implementation of the Rules to examine the adequacy of the basic
infrastructure, proper security arrangements and other facilities at Hostels and
Ashrams. The Hostels and Ashrams were running in private and rented
building without having basic facilities like bath room, toilet, over head tanks,
boundary wall, electricity, and alternative power. In the test checked districts,
fortnightly health checkups could not be provided to 39 per cent students,

xi


eval, Social and Economic (Non-PSUs) Sectors.

far the year ended 31 March 2013

Computer training was not imparted to any of the students during last two
years and special coaching on difficult subjects was not imparted to all
hostellers except students of class 8" and 10". Food stuff register in support of
proper utilisation of stipend money were not maintained, Security arrangement
for hostellers were not made and women police guards for girls hostels were
not deployed. The maintenance of records like visitors register, complaints
register etc. was very poor.

(Paragraph 2.7)

lisbursement of compensation the fami members of

The Jail Department is responsible for creation of a general fund to be kept in
Personal Deposit Account (PD account) in each district to deposit 50 per cent
of wages earned by convicted prisoners for disbursement to the victims or
dependent family members of the victims. We noticed that fund amounting to
% 4.64 crore had accumulated in the PD account mainly due to non-
identification of victims, refusal to accept the compensation by the victims,
death, migration and wrong address of the victims resulting in non
achievement of the very objective of fund to provide compensation to the
victims. Instances of non-reconciliation of account and parking of funds
outside the PD Account were also noticed. Further, non-holding of quarterly
meeting of the Committee resulted in the failure of the department to monitor
distribution of compensation to the victims or dependent family members of
the victims.

(Paragraph 2.8)

The Chhattisgarh Tourism Board (CTB) was responsible for development of
the basic tourism facility and to increase tourist inflow in Chhattisgarh.
Despite lapse of more than eight years, the CTB had completed only 16 out of
18 motels as of November 2013. Audit observed that inadequate planning at
initial stages and subsequent changes in scope of work at post tender stage led
to delay, cost overrun and non-completion of the motels. Failure of the CTB to
prepare a comprehensive plan for operation of these motels led to delay in
invitation of tender, non-handing over the motels even after tender and idling
of motels besides loss of revenue.

(Paragraph 2.9)

pliance Audit

Audit observed significant deficiencies in critical areas, which impact the
effectiveness of the State Government. Some important findings arising out of
compliance audit (nine paragraphs) are featured in the Report. The major
observations relate to non-compliance with rules and regulations, audit against
propriety and cases of expenditure without adequate justification and failure of

1.6 Planning and conduct of audit

The audit process commences with the assessment of risk of various
Government departments/ organisations/ autonomous bodies and schemes/
projects etc., based on criticality/complexity of activities, level of delegated
financial powers, internal controls, concerns of stakeholders and previous
audit findings. Based on this risk ssment, the frequency and extent of audit
are decided and an Annual Audit Plan is formulated.

After completion of audit, Inspection Report containing audit findings is
issued to the head of the office with request to furnish replies within one
month. Whenever replies are received, audit findings are either settled/ or
further action for compliance is advised. The important audit observations
pointed out in these Inspection Reports are processed for inclusion in the
Audit Reports of the Comptroller and Auditor General of India, which are
submitted to the Governor of Chhattisgarh under Article 151 of the
Constitution of India.

During 2012-13, compliance audit of 309 drawing and disbursing officers of
the State and one autonomous body was conducted by the office of the
Accountant General (Audit), Chhattisgarh. Besides, five Performance Audits
were also conducted.

Lack of respo: f Government to Inspection Reports

The Accountant General (Audit), Chhattisgarh conducts periodical inspection
of Government Departments by test-check of transactions and verify the
maintenance of important accounting and other records as per the prescribed
rules and procedures. These inspections are followed by issue of Audit



Audit Report on General, Social and Economic (Non-PSUs) Sectors for the year ended 31 March 2013

Inspection Reports (IRs). When important irregularities, etc., detected during
audit inspection are not settled on the spot, these IRs are issued to the heads of
offices inspected, with a copy to the next higher authorities.

The heads of offices and next higher authorities are required to report their
compliance to the AG (Audit) within four weeks of receipt of IRs.

Based on the results of test audit, the outstanding Inspections Reports
/Paragraphs as on 31st March 2013 are given in Table-1.4.

Table-1.4: Outstanding Inspection Reports/Paragraphs

Amount

olved
( in crore)

[1 | General 233 1065 419.48
2 Social 1889 4446 373.17
3 Economic (Non-PSUs) | 988 | 3547 5198.55

During 2012-13, two meetings of the Audit Committee were held in which 24
IRs and 155 paragraphs were settled.

The departmental officers failed to take action on observations contained in
IRs within the prescribed time frame resulting in erosion of accountability.

It is recommended that the Government may look into the matter to ensure
prompt and proper response to audit observations,

In the last few years, Audit has reported on several significant deficiencies in
implementation of various programmes/ activities as well as on the quality of
internal controls in selected departments, which have negative impact on the
success of programmes and functioning of the departments. The focus was on
auditing the specific programmes/ schemes and to offer suitable
recommendations to the executive for taking corrective action and improving
service delivery to the citizens.

As per the provision of Comptroller and Auditor General of India’s
Regulations on Audit and Accounts, 2007, the departments are required to
send their responses to draft Performance Audit reports/ draft paragraphs
proposed for inclusion in the Comptroller and Auditor General of India’s
Audit Reports within one month, It was brought to their personal attention that
in view of likely inclusion of such paragraphs in the Reports of the
Comptroller and Auditor General of India, to be placed before the State
Legislature, it would be desirable to include their comments in the matter.
They were also advised to have meeting with the Accountant General to
discuss the draft reports of Performance Audits and draft audit paragraphs.
These draft reports and paragraphs proposed for inclusion in the Report were
also forwarded to the Additional Chief Secretaries/ Principal Secretaries/
Secretaries concerned for seeking their replies. For the present Audit Report,
draft reports on five Performance Audits, four long paragraph and nine draft
paragraphs were forwarded to the concerned Administrative Secretaries. But
Government reply has been received in 13 cases only.



Chapter I:Introduction

1.9 Follow-up on Audit Reports

According to the Rules of procedure for the internal working of the Committee
on Public Accounts, the Administrative Departments were to initiate, suo motu
action on all Audit Paragraphs and Reviews featuring in the Comptroller and
Auditor General’s Audit Reports (ARs) regardless of whether these are taken
up for examination by the Public Accounts Committee or not. They were also
to furnish detailed notes, duly vetted by audit indicating the remedial action
taken or proposed to be taken by them within six months of the presentation of
the ARs to the State Legislature.

The position regarding receipt of Action taken Notes (ATNs) on the
paragraphs included in the ARs up to the period ended 31 March 2012 as on
31 August 2013 is given in Table-1.5.

AGRICULTURE DEPA

AEN

2.1 Rashtriya Kr

Executive Summary

Concerned by the slow growth in the Agriculture and allied sectors, the
National Development Council (NDC) in its meeting held on 29 May, 2007,
resolved to launch a special Additional Central Assistance Scheme Rashtriya
Krishi Vikas Yojana (RKVY) to incentivize States to draw up plans for their
agriculture sector more comprehensively. The NDC affirmed its commitment
to achieve four per cent annual growth in the agricultural sector during the
XI" plan.

The Performance Audit of RKVY was conducted for the period 2007-13 to
examine the effectiveness of Planning process, timely availability of funds and
their effective and economic utilisation, achievement of intended objectives of
the projects and efficiency and effectiveness of monitoring process. The major
audit findings are discussed below:

* There was delay in preparation and submission of District Agriculture
Plans and Comprehensive State Agriculture Plan (SAP). Projects costing

% 32.23 crore were implemented without inclusion in the SAP.
(Paragraph 2.1.6.1, 2.1.6.2 and 2.1.6.3)

* In 64 per cent cases of release of funds by the State Government to the
Nodal agencies, there was delay ranging from 10 to 145 days.
(Paragraph 2.1.7.2)

* Nurseries established at a cost of = 1.80 crore remained under-utilised as
plant production ranged from one to 49 per cent of production capacity.
(Paragraph 2.1.8.4)

* Construction of 112 Farmers Information Centers and 20 Block Level
Multipurpose Farmers Service Centers could not be taken up and
construction of 29 Godowns was incomplete due to which the intended
benefits could not be availed by beneficiaries.

(Paragraph 2.1.8.8)



Audit Report on General, Social and Economic (Non-PSUs) Sectors for the year ended 31 March 2013

* Slow progress and non-utilisation of funds under Rainfed Area
Development Programme resulted in non-release of further funds under
the Programme by Government of India.

(Paragraph 2.1.8.14)

Internal Audit wing did not conduct any separate audit of projects under
RKVY and no evaluation system was put in place by the State Level
Sanctioning Committee to ensure economic, efticient and eftective
implementation of RKVY projects.

(Paragraph 2.1.9.1 and 2.1.9.3)

2.1.1 Introduction

In the State of Chhattisgarh, about 35 per cent of the geographical area is
under cultivation by 33 lakh farmers, 76 per cent of whom belong to small and
marginal category. The cultivated area in Chhattisgarh is spread over three
Agro-climatic zones of Chhattisgarh Plains, Northern Hills and Bastar Plateau.
Irrigation is available for 27 per cent of the cultivated area’.

Concerned by the slow growth in the Agriculture and allied sectors, the
National Development Council (NDC) launched (29th May, 2007) a special
Additional Central Assistance Scheme known as National Agriculture
Development Programme (NADP), also known as Rashtriya Krishi Vikas
Yojana (RKVY) with the aim of achieving four per cent growth in Agricultural
Sector during XI Five Year Plan period (2007-2012). The NDC resolved that
agricultural development strategies must be reoriented to meet the needs of
farmers and called upon the Central and State Governments to evolve a
strategy lo rejuvenate agriculture.

In Chhattisgarh, various Central and State Schemes are being implemented
such as National Food Security Mission (NFSM), Integrated Scheme of
Pulses, Oilseeds and Maize (ISOPOM), Agriculture Technology Management
Agency Scheme (ATMA), Shakambari, Krishak Samagra Vikas Yojana
(KSVY), Rajya Ganna Vikas Yojana, Laghu Sinchaayi Yojana, etc., for
development of agriculture. The State achieved more than six per cent growth
in the agricultural sector during the XIth Plan period (2007-2012) against the
targeted growth of four per cent as envisaged in the RKVY guidelines.

2.1.2

isational setup

The Ministry of Agriculture, Department of Agriculture and Cooperation
(DAC), Government of India (Gol) is responsible for budgetary controls,
release of funds and overall administration of the scheme at the Central level.
The State Agriculture Department is the Nodal department for the
implementation of the scheme in the State.

The Nodal department finalises the State Agricultural Plan (SAP) and submits
it to the State Planning Department for integrating with the overall State Plans

1. Source: The State Agriculture Plan (2007-12)



Chapt

:Performance Audit

who will pose it to the Planning Commission as a part of its State Plan
exercise. On approval of SAP, the Nodal Department places the Stream-I?
proposals before the State Level Sanctioning Committee (SLSC) which is
constituted for sanctioning of the projects at State Level and to review the
implementation of the scheme, objectives and to ensure that the projects are
implemented in accordance with the guidelines laid down by the Central
Government. DAC releases the funds to the Nodal department after the SLSC
sanctions the projects.

The performance audit of RKVY was taken up with the objective to assess
whether:

* The planning process for implementation of the scheme was effective and

according to the RKVY guidelines;

The financial management was adequate and funds were made available
for effective and economic utilisation;

The projects were implemented according to the regulatory structure in
place and the intended objectives of the projects were achieved and Nodal
Department effectively coordinated with various departments and
implementing agencies for implementing various projects;

Internal control mechanisms were put in place to ensure efficient and
effective monitoring and control over implementation; monitoring
mechanism at each level was adequate.

The funds under RKVY are available to the States in two distinct streams. At least
75 per cent of the allocated funds shall be proposed under Stream-I for specific projects

and funds under Stream-II shall be used for strengthening the existing State sector
schemes for filling the resource gaps.



Audit Report on General, Social and Economic (Non-PSUs) Sectors for the year ended 31 March 2013

The following were the sources of the audit criteria:

* Guidelines for Rashtriya Krishi Vikas Yojana (RKVY) of Department of
Agriculture and Cooperation, Ministry of Agriculture.

* Information provided by the Department in the website of RKVY scheme
titled ‘rkvy.nic.in’.

* Economic Survey Report for the year 2012-13.

* Evaluation studies by National Institute of Rural Development (NIRD)

* Comprehensive State Agricultural Plan.

* Guidelines for projects under implementation.

* Instructions/guidelines issued at the State/District level for
implementation of RKVY scheme.

* — State General Financial and Accounts Rules.

2.1.5 Scope of audit and audit methodology

The Performance Audit was conducted covering the period from 2007-08 to
2012-13 which involved audit of the records of Agriculture Department
(Nodal Department for implementation of RKVY in the State), Horticulture
Department, Cooperative Department, Chhattisgarh Rajya Beej Evam Krishi
Vikas Nigam, Chhattisgarh State Agriculture Marketing (Mandi) Board, State
Agriculture University and Agriculture Engincering Office.

A total of 19 out of 454 projects under Stream-I covering nine’ sectors
(as listed in Appendix 2.1.1) weve selected and two out of five projects under
Stream-II were selected. Besides these, two sub-schemes were also selected.
For cach selected project, six districts were selected for the Performance
Audit. Physical verification of some projects was also done in addition to the
field audit.

An entry conference to discuss the audit objectives and the audit criteria
adopted for the Performance Audit was held with the Agriculture Production
Commissioner cum Principal Secretary, Agriculture Department (APC) on
31 May 2013. Government furnished (December 2013) replies to the audit
findings which have been suitably incorporated wherever necessitated. The
audit findings were also discussed with the APC in the Exit Conference held
on 28 December 2013.

Audit findings

2.1.6 Planning

As per para 3.1 and 3.2 of the RKVY guidelines, each District should
formulate a District Agriculture Plan (DAP) by including the resources

$Crop, Horticulture, Co-operative, Seed, Extension, Agriculture Mechanisation, Agriculture
Research and Marketing and Other.

available from the existing District, State or Central schemes. The DAPs
should project the requirements for development of Agriculture and allied
sectors of the district including animal husbandry, fishery, minor irrigation
projects, rural development works, agricultural marketing schemes and
schemes for water harvesting and conservation etc for the next five years.
A comprehensive State Agricultural Plan (SAP) integrating all the DAPs
should be prepared at the State level. The DAPs were to be prepared in
2007-08. Further, as per para 3.3 of the RKVY guidelines, a clear indication
should be given by the States that they are encouraging the preparation of the
DAP and from 2008-09 onwards, no assistance under the RKVY would be
available unless all the districts are ready with the DAPs.

2.1.6.1 Delayed submission of Comprehensive-State Agriculture Plan

From scrutiny of the records of Nodal Office, we observed that although the
DAPs were to be prepared during 2007-08, the DAPs pertaining to 13 districts
were actually prepared in December 2009 and for five districts the same were
prepared in January 2010. The comprehensive SAP was also prepared only in
March 2010 and submitted to DAC, Gol in May 2010. Thus there was
inordinate delay in preparation and submission of DAPs and SAP.

Although there was delay in preparation and submission of the SAP, National
Institute of Rural Development (NIRD) in its analytical report had mentioned
that the same was well prepared but needs further improvement in terms of
information on sources of funding, experience of implementing
earlier/ongoing schemes, etc.

On this being pointed out (October 2013), the Government stated
(December 2013) that after the meeting (January 2008) of the State Level
Sanctioning Committee (SLSC) for sanctioning the projects for 2007-08,
funds were released and action initiated for preparation of DAP. Since
experienced officials were not available with the Department, consultants were
appointed after inviting Expression of Interest. The selection process took
nearly 12 months after which the work of preparation of DAP was
commenced. Since considerable time was consumed for compilation of the
data, the time schedule prescribed by Gol for submission of DAP and SAP
could not be adhered. It was also stated that Gol considered the practical
problems faced during the preparation of DAP and had released funds during
2009-10.

The delayed preparation of DAP and SAP however led to sanction of projects
without their inclusion in SAP as discussed in the subsequent paragraphs.

2.1.6.2 Non-inclusion of projects in DAPs and SAP
(i) As per instructions issued by the DAC, Gol during the meeting of the

SLSC (17 September 2008), projects already sanctioned for the year 2007-08
and 2008-09 should compulsorily be included in the DAPs.

We observed from the DAPs of the selected Districts that the projects
sanctioned by SLSC for the years 2007-08 and 2008-09 were not included in
the DAPs. The DAPs prepared by the Districts did not include project
proposals for the years 2007-08 and 2008-09 and only included project



Projects
costing हैं 31.48
crore were
implemented
without
inclusion in the
SAP.

Audit Report on General, Social and Economic (Non-PSUs) Sectors for the year ended 31 March 2013

proposals for the year 2009-10 to 2011-12. The same were submitted to Gol in
March 2010. Thus, the projects sanctioned by SLSC during the years 2007-08
and 2008-09 were not actually included in the SAP indicating that there was
lack of planning in implementation of the scheme.

On this being pointed (October 2013) out, Government stated (December
2013) that since the work plans for the years 2007-08 and 2008-09 were
sanctioned by SLSC even before the preparation of DAP, they were not
included in the proposals. However, the projects were as per the requirement.

Action of the Government was not as per the Gol instructions which stated
that the sanctioned projects of the years 2007-08 and 2008-09 were to be
included in the DAPs.

(ii) Scrutiny of projects approved by SLSC during the period 2007-08 to
2012-13 revealed that the project ‘Interim relief to farmers for damage of
Hybrid Rice KRH-2’ was proposed by the Beej Nigam. The said project was
implemented (2010-11) at a cost of ¥ 22.03 crore after obtaining sanction from
SLSC under Stream-I. However, the same was not found included in the SAP
indicating that the project was not proposed by the Districts through their
DAPs.

Further, eight projects proposed by Indira Gandhi Agriculture University
(IGAU) were sanctioned (29 May 2010) by SLSC for हैं 9.45 crore
(Appendix 2.1.2) under Stream- of RKVY which were implemented by
IGAU during the year 2010-11. However, these projects were actually not
proposed in the SAP. Sanction and implementation of these projects without
having proposed in SAP and incurring expenditure on them was irregular.

During the Exit Conference (December 2013), Agriculture Production
Commissioner cum Principal Secretary (APC) while accepting the facts, stated
that though the projects were not included in the SAP, the same were however,
sanctioned by the SLSC which is the competent authority. APC further stated
that in future, efforts would be made that only those projects which have been
proposed in SAP would be submitted to SLSC. However, keeping in view the
exigency, other proposals which are not included in SAP may be considered
by the SLSC.

Implementation of projects without inclusion in SAP indicated lack of
planning in selection of projects.

2.1.6.3 Sanction of project without proper planning led to non-utilisation
of Grant

Chhattisgarh Beej Evam Krishi Vikas Nigam (Beej Nigam) takes seed
production program at farmers’ field and procures certified seeds from them.
In case the farmers’ seeds are declared failed by seed certification agency,
they would not be getting the support price in Mandi. In order to mitigate loss
to farmers, it was proposed to reimburse the loss on failed seeds from RK VY
through Beej Nigam. For the year 2011-12, the proposal for ‘Grant on Fail
seeds’ was sanctioned by SLSC (April 2011).



Grant on fail
seed of [75
lakh could not
be utilised for
the intended
purpose.

Chapter 2:Perf

ance Audit

Scrutiny of the records of Managing Director (MD), Beej Nigam revealed that
during 2011-12, Beej Nigam was granted हैं 75 lakh for this purpose
However, instead of Beej Nigam, Chhattisgarh Marketing Federation
(MARKFED), a Nodal agency for procurement of paddy and distribution of
fertilizers in Chhattisgarh, had procured the failed seeds during 2011-12. The
grant of € 75 lakh sanctioned to Beej Nigam thus could not be utilised till
March 2012 and was adjusted in the subsequent year’s (2012-13) sanctions.
Thus sanction of grant and its subsequent non utilisation by the designated
agency is indicative of improper planning. We further observed that the
project ‘Grant on fail seeds’ was not included in the SAP and as such sanction
accorded by the SLSC was irregular.

On this being pointed (October 2013) out, Government stated (December
2013) that since the State Government had decided to purchase the failed seed
from farmers through MARKFED, the amount sanctioned to the Beej Nigam
could not be utilised. As regards non-inclusion of the project in SAP, APC
stated during the Exit Conference (December 2013) that in the SAP there is
provision for seed related activities. Since all the activities cannot be described
in the SAP in detail, the project of Grant on failed seed was not mentioned
separately in the SAP. In future, due care would be taken to undertake the
projects included in SAP.

2.1.6.4 Improper planning led to non-utilisation of हैं 11.45 lakh sanctioned
Sor training infrastructure

For effective implementation of the project, it is an essential pre-requisite to
develop the capacity of the personnel both at the service providers and
teceivets levels. To develop infrastructure, construction of Training cum
demonstration centre was proposed (March 2009) by DHFF.

During test check of records of the DHFF, we however observed that an
amount of हैं 11.45 lakh was sanctioned (March 2009) under RKVY and
provided (April 2010) to the Mandi Board for construction of Training cum
demonstration centre at Jagdalpur, Bastar. However, the work was not
commenced till October 2013.

On this being pointed out (October 2013), Government stated (December
2013) that despite several reminders, Mandi Board did not commence the
work. Hence, on the direction of Collector, Bastar District, Mandi Board
refunded (April 2013) the allotted funds for transferring to other executing
agencies.

Fact however remains that non-commencement of the work of the Training
cum demonstration centre even after nearly five years is not only indicative of
improper planning and monitoring by the Department but also resulted in non-
achievement of the intended objective.

At least 75 per cent of the allocated funds shall be proposed under Stream-I
for specific projects. The funds under Stream-IL will be available for

2.1.7.1 Release of funds and position of Utilisation Certificates

As per para 7.2.4 of the RKVY guidelines, the Nodal agency is required to
ensure that the Central Assistance released under the scheme is utilised in
accordance with the approved SAP and DAPs. The release of the second and
final installments of the allocation depends upon the progress of utilization of
funds. The State should ensure that the funds released are utilized promptly
and properly and progress reports are sent to the DAC at the earliest.
Non-utilisation of Central assistance could hinder further release of funds.

The detail of funds released to the State under RKVY and status of Utilisation
Certificates (UC) issued during the period 2007-08 to 2012-13 are indicated in

2.1.7.2 Delay in release of funds to Nodal department

Effective implementation of the projects depends upon timely release of funds.
It was observed that there was delay in release of funds by the State
Government to the Nodal department during the period 2007-08 to 2012-13.
Out of the total 77 instances of fund released to the Nodal agencies, in
49 instances (64 per cent) the delay ranged between 10 and 145 days as
detailed in Appendix 2.1.3.

APC, while accepting the audit observation during Exit Conference
(December 2013) stated that the release of funds will be streamlined to avoid
inordinate delay in future.

There is a need to streamline the release of funds for effective implementation
of the projects.

2.1.7.3 Non-reconciliation of data relating to funds released under
Stream-I

Under RKVY, States are permitted to initiate specific projects with definite
time-lines and clear objectives for Agriculture and allied sectors. For this
purpose, the RKVY would be available to the States under Stream-I and
Stream-II. The RK VY Guidelines (3.2) states that the entire responsibility for
proper implementation of RKVY shall be that of the Agriculture Department.

Even though State Agriculture Department is the Nodal Agency for
implementation of RKVY in Chhattisgarh, the funds are however, allotted by
GoCG directly to the various implementing agencies

Scrutiny of the records of Nodal Officer’ and as per information furnished by
GoCG, during the period 2007-08 to 2012-13, funds amounting ¥ 262.98 crore
were released by Gol under Stream-II of RKVY to GoCG as detailed below:

2.1.8 Implementation of schemes

For the purpose of Performance Audit, a total of 19 projects covering nine
sectors under Stream-I, two projects under Stream-LL and two sub-schemes
were selected for detailed checking. The selected projects and sectors are
shown in Appendix 2.1.1.

Crop Sector

Following projects were audited under Crop Sector:

1. DNA fingerprinting, bio-prospecting of genes and allele mining of CG
germplasm (core collection) for various biotic, abiotic, yield and grain
quality traits

2. Tissue Culture Laboratory, Raipur

We in audit observed that the target of establishment of infrastructure and
technology for testing the genetic purity of hybrid rice, setting up the seed
bank, DNA fingerprinting of elite germplasm etc., was completed (March
2012) and 31 samples of hybrid seeds were tested, 30 DNA fingerprinting and
56 germ-plasm lines with various traits were identified as of July 2013.
Shortcoming noticed in the project of Tissue Culture Laboratory is discussed
in the following paragraph:

2.1.8.1 Non-functioning of the Tissue Culture Laboratory at Raipur

For production of tissue culture plants for commercial purpose, Gol
sanctioned (2005-06 and 2006-07) % 1.55 crore under Macro Management of
Agriculture, a centrally sponsored scheme, for construction of Tissue Culture
Lab at Raipur having an annual production capacity of 10 lakh plants. Further,
under RKVY, an amount of % 3.72 crore was sanctioned (2010-11) for the said
lab. Besides this, amount of हैं 75.00 lakh was sanctioned in 2010-11 under
RKVY for creating a revolving fund for running and functioning of the lab.

We observed that the civil construction of Tissue Culture lab building was
completed (June 2010) and equipments and related items were installed
(July 2012) in the lab. A total of हैं 3.34 crore was incurred on the
establishment of tissue culture lab.

The lab was transferred (April 2013) to the Department of Plant Molecular
Biology and Biotechnology, Indira Gandhi Krishi Vishwavidhyalaya
(IGKVV) for operation. However, we observed that the Tissue Culture lab
could not be made operational till date (September 2013) due to want of
electric connection. Due to non-operational of the Tissue culture lab, the
production of the tissue culture plants could not be commenced since more
than one and a half years despite the fact that construction of building and
procurement of equipments were completed by December 2010 and
equipments were installed by July 2012. Thus, the infrastructure created at a
cost of = 3.34 crore remained idle for 17 months (as of December 2013).
Further, the amount of हैं 75 lakh released for creating a revolving fund
remained un-utilised since 2010-11.



Audit Report on General, Social and Economic (Non-PSUs) Sectors for the year ended 31 March 2013

During the Exit Conference (December 2013), APC accepted the audit
observation and stated that electric connection would be established soon and
the laboratory would be made functional at the earliest.

Non-operation of the tissue culture lab not only resulted in non-utilisation of
the created infrastructure but also resulted in non-utilisation of = 75 lakh.

2.1.8.3 Underutilization of the infrastructure created in nurseries

The State Plan of Horticulture Sector (2008-09 to 2010-11) mentions that
despite having more than 100 existing nurseries in the Horticulture
Department, there have been shortage of planting stock and each year, huge
amount is being spent on purchase of plants from other sources. Looking into
the gap between demand and supply of the planting materials, establishment of
nurseries was planned to ensure timely supply of quality planting materials.

As pet Para 8.6 of National Horticulture Mission® (NHM) guidelines, the
model nurseries to be established under the Public Sector were eligible for
100 per cent assistance subject to a maximum of हैं 18 lakh per unit for a
maximum area of four hectares. The nurseries established will be required to
produce one lakh plants per hectare per year. Since the implementation of
projects under Horticulture sector would be done as per the guidelines and
norms of NHM, the nurseries established under RK VY were also required to
produce the minimum number of plants.

Under RKVY, SLSC sanctioned (January 2008) the project of Support for
Establishment of Nurseries and Maintenance (Project ID 134) for ¥ 2.16 crore.
This included establishment of 12 Model nurseries at हैं 18 lakh each. Funds
also provided to six districts for establishing two nurseries in each
ict during the year 2007-08.

Test check of records of 10 out of these 12 nurseries revealed that in these
nurseries production of plants ranged from one to 49 per cent of production
capacity, indicating under-utilisation of the infrastructure created at a cost of
% 1.80 crore’ as detailed in Appendix 2.1.4. Details of plant production called
for (July 2013) in respect of two model nurseries (Gaurabeda and Pegdapalli)
established in Bijapur district, were awaited (January 2014)

During the Exit Conference (December 2013), APC while accepting the audit
observation, cited electricity and labour problems as the reasons for low
production of plants.

(September 2008) that the implementation of projects under Horticulture
should be done as per the guidelines and norms of NHM. It was further
directed (September 2008 and July 2009) that the projects sanctioned under
RKVY would be implemented only in non-NHM Districts.

A project for ‘Establishment of new nurseries’ was sanctioned
(September 2008) for = 2.88 crore under RKVY. Accordingly, SLSC
sanctioned eight new nurseries in the State. Out of this, seven nurseries were
established and one nursery in Bastar district was not taken up due to

2.1.8.6 Irregular establishment of Floriculture Unit costing € 1.75 crore and
irregular expenditure of € 16.25 lakh on fertigation and filteration system

(i) With the objective to motivate farmers for flower cultivation, introduction
of new flower varieties in the State, promotion of cut flowers of Dendrobium,
Anthurium, Rose and Gerbera farming in the region for production of high
quality cut flowers etc., the proposal for establishment of Floriculture Unit
under controlled conditions was sanctioned by SLSC for the year 2008-09 at a
cost of = 1.70 crore. As per the proposal, the unit was to be established in the
departmental nursery in Baster district. The Floriculture Unit will have the
impact of production of cut-flowers under artificial environment.

Scrutiny of records of Assistant Director, Horticulture (ADH), Principal
Garden (PG), Raipur, revealed that work order was issued by the DHFF?
(January 2009) to North Eastern Regional Agricultural Marketing Cooperation
Limited (NERAMAC) for ‘Turnkey project of floriculture development under
controlled condition’ to be established in 3200 sqm area at Raipur. For this,
shade net house, poly house, fogging system, roof screen, drip irrigation, cold
room, etc., were installed during the period February 2009 to June 2009 at
Sokranalla Nursery, Raipur after incurring expenditure of हैं 1.75 crore.
Although the SLSC had sanctioned the Floriculture unit for Jagdalpur, the
same was established at Raipur (June 2010) for which no sanction from the
appropriate authority was available on records.

Scrutiny of records further revealed that the floriculture unit established at
Sokranalla was required to produce 1,00,000 Dendrobium, 15,000 Anthurium,
72,000 Rose and 1,20,000 Gerbera plants annually. However against the
production capacity, actual production was far less and ranged between one
per cent and 12 per cent as detailed below:

(ii) Scrutiny of the records of ADH, PG, Raipur revealed that the items to be

Seed Sector

Following projects were audited under Seed Sector

1. Incentive to Seed Growers

2. Interim relief to farmers for damage of Hybrid Rice KRH-2
We in audit observed that the project of Interim Relief to farmers for damage
of Hybrid Rice KRH-2 was not taken in the SAP and this aspect has been
covered in Paragraph 2.1.6.2(ii). Relief to farmers aggregating हैं 22.03 crore
was provided (2011-12) for a total of 2088 quintals of damaged rice.
Shortcomings noticed in implementation of the project of Incentive to Seed
Growers are discussed in the following paragraph.

2.1.8.7 Payment of subsidy on ineligible varieties of seeds

In Chhattisgarh, although the seed production has been increasing year after
year, the demand is increasing in higher proportion resulting in a wide gap
between production and distribution. This widening gap clearly indicates that
farmers are getting more and more inclined to use certified seed. The Gol
provides assistance to States under Macro Management of Agriculture (MMA)
and National Food Security Mission (NFSM) to encourage distribution of
certified seed varieties not older than 10 years

An amount of % seven crore was sanctioned by the SLSC under RKVY in
2008-09 for the project ‘Incentive to Seed growers’. The seed production
incentive of < 200 per quintal were to be provided to the farmers on tagged
seeds procured by the Beej Nigam only. As per directions (October 2010) of
the Gol, incentive to seed growers is to be made available for newly released
varieties only. However, incentive can also be provided for some listed
varieties which are better performing irrespective of whether less than or more
than 10 years old.

23


Incentive of T one
crore was
provided for seed
production on
ineligible varieties
of seeds.

Audit Report on General, Social and Economic (Noa-PSUs) Sectors for the. ded 31 March 2013

Scrutiny of records related to seed procurement at the processing centre/farms
of the Beej Nigam, we observed that during the year 2008-09, 2.46,013.97
quintals of paddy (24 varieties) and 35,589.64 quintals of wheat seeds (24
varieties) were procured for which incentive of % 5.63 crore (% 4.92 crore +
0.71 crore) was paid to the farmers. Out of these varieties, 19 varieties of
rice (38,120.30 quintals) and 19 varieties of wheat (11,954.44 quintals) were
more than 10 years old and were not listed varieties. Thus, incentive of = one
crore (Appendix 2.1.5) provided for seed production under RKVY on
ineligible varieties of seeds was inadmissible. In the selected districts,
incentive amounting to % 73.12 lakh was paid on the ineligible varieties.

It was further revealed that as per sanction of SLSC, incentive was to be
provided to farmers for seed production of wheat and paddy. It was however
revealed that incentive aggregating € 48.96 lakh was paid to the farmers for
pulses (4545.30 quintals) and millets (kodo and ragi) (100.95 quintals) at the
rate of हैं 1000 per quintal for pulses and = 800 per quintal for millets. This
resulted in release of incentive amounting to हैं 48.96 lakh on inadmissible
items.

On this being pointed (October 2013) out, Government stated (January 2014)
that in the proposal for incentive on seed production, there was no restriction
of 10 year old variety. Further, it was also stated that incentive is provided
only on those varieties which are prevalent and demanded by farmers. In
respect of providing incentive for distribution of pulses and millets, Manager
(Production), Beej Nigam stated (July 2013) that the RK VY guidelines clearly
mentions that the work plan should be prepared as per the State’s and local
needs.

Reply of the Government is not acceptable as incentive was to be provided
only on newer varieties of seeds or on those seeds which were listed, as was
reiterated in the Gol instructions (October 2010). Further, under MMA and
NFSM, distribution of certified seed varieties not older than 10 years is
encouraged. Department’s contention on distribution of pulses and millets is
not acceptable as even though RKVY guidelines mentions for preparation of
work plan as per the State’s and local needs, the same should have been
approved by the SLSC.

10 godowns were constructed by the constructing agency (District
Co-operative Bank) after incurring expenditure of हैं 50.49 lakh leaving
balance of € 14.51 lakh (% 65 lakh - हैं 50.49 lakh) which was not returned till
date (October 2013). In Rajnandgaon district, we observed that out of the
savings of हैं 21.44 lakh on the construction of godowns sanctioned during
2009-10 to 2011-12, three additional godowns were constructed as per the
directions of the District Collector. However, approval for the same was not
obtained from the SLSC.

Since the main objective of providing godowns in cooperative sector was to
increase their storage facilities, non-completion of the godowns therefore led
to only partial-achievement of the objective as the Department could achieve
30200 MT storage capacities against the target of 51000 MT under RKVY.

On this being pointed (October 2013) out, Government stated (December
2013) that for the construction of FICs, BMFCs and 100 MT Godowns, the
land is allotted by the Revenue Department. To ascertain the availability and
feasibility of the land, time is consumed. After the selection of the land,
tenders are invited by Mandi Board, PWD or RES and negotiations are done
for the lowest rates. Further, construction works get hindered during the rainy
season. All these led to non-completion of the buildings within prescribed
time. It was further stated that for speeding up the infrastructure development
works, Government had directed the District Collectors to review the works.

Reply of the Government is not acceptable as all the reasons cited by the
Government should have been considered before taking up the work.

2.1.8.10 Irregular payment of assistance on Power operated agriculture
implements.

As per para-4(b) of RKVY guidelines, assistance is provided for purchase of
agriculture implements and machinery for enhancing farm productivity. As per
para 5.5.2 of the guidelines issued (August 2008) by GoCG for providing farm
equipments to farmers, the physical ver ion report, bill and warranty card
of the machine/equipment should indicate clearly the serial number of the
machine/equipment.

Scrutiny of records of DDA, Durg revealed that during 2011-13, assistance of
र₹ 14.81 lakh was released for power tillers and other power-driven farm
equipments without ensuring the availability of the engine or chassis number
in the bills which was irregular. We did not notice such irregularities in other
test checked districts.

On this being pointed (October 2013) out, Government stated (December
2013) that the manufacturer’s equipments have engine and chassis numbers
which are physically verified before releasing the assistance to farmers.

Reply of the Government is not acceptable as the engine and chassis numbers
were not found mentioned in the bill and warranty cards as was required in the
guidelines,

2.1.8.11 Overlapping of activities under RKVY and Macro Management of
Agriculture scheme

As per Para 6.3 of RKVY Guidelines, the SLSC is responsible for sanctioning
the projects under Stream-I of the RKVY and to ensure that no duplication of
efforts or resources takes place. Gol further advised (July 2010) the State
Government to ensure that there would be no duplication/overlapping of
activities/areas covered under RKVY Stream-[ projects vis-a-vis existing
schemes of Central or State Government.

During scrutiny of records of the schemes in the test checked districts, it was
observed that in Durg, Bilaspur and Rajnandgaon districts, istance to
farmers for various agricultural equipments/machineries like tractors, power
tillers, power operated/driven agricultural implements etc., valuing
र₹ 13.54 crore (Appendix 2.1.8) were provided under RKVY. It was also
observed that assistance to farmers for the same components were provided in
these districts even under Macro Management of Agriculture (MMA). Thus,
there was overlapping of activities under RKVY which was in contravention



Chapter 2:Performance Audit

of the provisions of guidelines issued by Gol.

On this being pointed (October 2013), Government stated (December 2013)
that the guidelines of RKVY clearly empowers the SLSC to sanction projects
as per the requirement of the State. Due to insufficient funds under MMA,
funds from RKVY were also provided for giving assistance to farmers on
improved farm machineries. [t was further stated that since both schemes are
beneliciary oriented, farmers are provided assistance for a particular activity
only from any one scheme.

The fact remains that expenditure from RKVY funds on activities already
covered under MMA scheme in these districts was in violation of RKVY
guidelines.

well as make purchases of their requirements. The infrastructure at all these
places of marketing is quite meager and needs strengthening.

2.1.8.14 Non-utilisation of funds under Rainfed Area Development
Programme led to non-release of further funds by Gol

With an aim to improve the quality of life of farmers’ by offering a complete
package of acti es to maximise the farm returns, the Rainfed Area
Development Programme (RADP) was started by Gol during the year 2010-11
as a sub-scheme of RKVY. RADP is based on holistic agriculture
development through Integrated Farming System (IFS) which provides an
opportunity for development and integration of multiple components of
agricultural system such as crops, horticulture, livestock, fishery etc.

Scrutiny of the records of the Nodal Officer revealed that during the year
2011-12, funds amounting % 15 crore was sanctioned (April 2011) under the
sub-scheme by SLSC against which amount of हैं 7.50 crore was released
(August 2011) as first installment. Sanction of the second installment was
subject to the performance and response of the State towards the programme.
As only हैं 62.04 lakh (eight per cent) was utilised by the State till December
2011, Gol delayed the release of the second installment of € 7.50 crore which
was subsequently released (March 2012) at the fag end of the financial year.
Against the allotted funds, expenditure of = 6.23 crore could only be utilised
for development of 2049 hectares (ha) against the target of 3478 ha by the end
of March 2012.

Audit observed that the achievement of various components at the end of
March 2013 under this Programme ranged from 39 to 133 per cent except
under Crop Insurance component where no insurance was provided against the
target of 70 hectares (Appendix 2.1.9). Due to slow progress in
implementation of the programme, funds totalling % 8.78 crore remained
unutilized at the end of 2011-12 and ¥ 2.91 crore at the end of 2012-13 leading

drills and construction of irrigation channels etc.

districts, expenditure of = 167.74 crore was incurred in NFSM districts also. In

incurred in Durg, हैं 24.93 crore in Sarguja, % 35.37 crore in Bilaspur,
% 6.42 crore in Rajnandgaon and % 29.12 crore in Raipur district which was

onitoring and Ev
2.1.9.1 Absence of monitoring of the implementation of RKVY

The guidelines of RKVY envisages constitution of a State Level Sanctioning
Committee (SLSC) which will be responsible for sanctioning of projects under
Stream-I of the RKVY, monitoring the progress of the sanctioned
projects/schemes, reviewing the implementation of the schemes’ objectives
and ensure that the programmes are implemented in accordance with the
guidelines laid down, ensuring that no duplication of efforts or resources takes
place, initiating evaluation studies from time to time, as may be required etc.
SLSC shall meet as often as required but shall meet at least once in a quarter.

As per amendments to the guidelines of the RKVY intimated by Gol
(July 2008), provision of constitution of a Committee under Chairmanship of
Agriculture Production Commissioner (APC) which would review the
implementation of RK VY on a monthly basis and submit reports to the SLSC
was added.

We however, observed trom the records of Nodal Office that since the
implementation of RKVY in the State (from 2007-08 to 2012-13) the SLSC
could meet only nine times against the required 24 times. Further, no
committee was constituted under the Chairmanship of APC to review the
implementation of RKVY on a monthly basis and as such, no review reports
on implementation of RKVY were furnished to the SLSC.

On this being pointed (October 2013) out, Government stated (December
2013) that monitoring of sanctioned projects is done during the SLSC
meetings. Further, it was also stated that consultant from National Institute of
Rural Development (NIRD) had also conducted a monitoring and APC also

38


Chapter 2:Performance ¢

reviews the progress during weekly meetings. At present, NABARD has been
appointed to conduct an end-term monitoring. During the Exit Conference
(December 2013), APC stated that efforts will be made to strengthen the
monitoring mechanism

The fact remains that the frequency of SLSC meetings was very less and also
that NIRD did not furnish any monitoring report to the State till date. The
Committee for reviewing the implementation of RKVY required under
Guidelines of Scheme was also not constituted.

2.1.9.2 Monitoring by National Institute of Rural Development

The National Institute of Rural Development (NIRD), Hyderabad was
appointed by Gol in the year 2007-08 as the Standing Consultant for
monitoring of RKVY and to assist the State in rendering support in planning/
implementation and monitoring of RKVY at local level.

Nodal Officer reported (July 2013) that consultants were appointed by NIRD
who reported the progress directly to Gol. However, at present no consultant
appointed either by NIRD or Gol was working in the State.

During the Exit Conference (December 2013), APC stated that since NIRD
was appointed by the Gol, they may have submitted their report to Gol.
Government stated (January 2014) that in future, review of the monitoring
activities conducted by NIRD would be regularly done.

As no monitoring reports were submitted to the GoCG by the consultants from
NIRD, Hyderabad, appropriate action on the short comings in implementation
of the scheme activities also could not taken by the Department.

2.1.9.3 Internal Control

Internal Control Mechanisms including internal audit are put in place to ensure
efficient and effective monitoring and control over implementation and to
ensure that the monitoring mechanism at each level was adequate.

Audit scrutiny revealed that:

* Inthe Agriculture Department, Internal Audit wing though established, did
not conduct any separate audit of the projects under RKVY scheme. In
the Horticulture Department, the Internal Audit wing was not established,
as such no internal audit of the Scheme was conducted till date

* As per Para 7.1.5 of the RKVY Guidelines, the Nodal Agency was to
ensure that an inventory of the assets created under the Projects should be
preserved and assets that are no longer required, should be transferred to
the Nodal Department, for its use and redeployment where possible.
However, no separate policy was found to have been formulated in the
State to safeguard and protect the assets created under RKVY.

2.1.10

‘ion

The State has implemented various Central and State Schemes for
development of agriculture besides Rashtriya Krishi Vikas Yojana (RKVY)
and was able to achieve more than six per cent growth during the XT" Five

39


Audit Report on General, Social and Economic (Non-PSUs) Sector the year ended 31 March 2013

Year Plan (2007-2012) period in the agriculture sector as compared to
3.93 per cent’! during the X" Five Year Plan (2002-2007). The efforts of the
Department in maximising returns to the farmers in Agriculture were very
encouraging as the productivity in respect of major crops increased
considerably and ranged from 12 to 35 per cent. RKVY was launched to
incentivise states to draw up plans for their agriculture sector more
comprehensively so as to achieve four per cent annual growth in the
agricultural sector during the XT" Plan by ensuring a holistic development of
Agriculture and allied sectors. However, some of the projects of RKVY
reviewed were completed within the time frame and some projects were
delayed or were incomplete. There was delay in preparation and submission of
District Agriculture Plans and State Agriculture Plan. Lack of planning in
implementation of the projects was observed as many projects were not
included in the State Agriculture Plan. There was delay in release of funds to
implementing agencies and funds were not utilised efficiently resulting in non-
release of subsequent funds from Government of India. Even though
substantial amount was shown as utilised, significant amount was retained in
Civil deposit and with the implementing agencies. We also observed instances
of non-completion of construction works and under-utilisation of created
infrastructure. Internal audit, a vital component of internal control mechanism,
was not done separately for RK VY projects by the Nodal department and was
absent in the Horticulture department. Monitoring of the scheme activities was
ineffective, frequency of State Level Sanctioning Committee meetings was
very less and committee required to be constituted as per Scheme guidelines to
review the implementation of RKVY was not constituted.

2.1.11 Recommen mn

We recommend that the Government should:-

* Ensure that there is synergy between the Projects sanctioned and
District Agriculture Plans and State Agriculture Plan.

* Ensure complete utilisation of funds to avoid subsequent non-allotment
of funds by Government of India

* Take steps to ensure achievement of capacity utilisation in plant
production by the nurseries (Horticulture).

* Initiate steps to complete the construction works of Farmers’
information centers, Block Level Multipurpose Farmers Centres and
Godowns at the earliest so that benefits of the scheme are availed by
targeted beneficiaries.

+ Organise meeting of State Level Sanctioning Committee at least once
in a quarter and constitute a Committee under Agriculture Production
Commissioner to review the implementation of Rashtriya Krishi Vikas
Yojana on a monthly basis.

Executive Summary

Indira Awaas Yojana (IAY), a flagship scheme of the Ministry of Rural
Development (MoRD), was launched with an aim to provide houses to the poor in
the rural areas. The Performance Audit of IAY was carried out to examine the
implementation of the scheme in the backdrop of its objectives to provide housing
for rural Below Poverty Line (BPL) homeless population and facilitate them to
have a complete functional dwelling unit with basic needs. The major audit
findings are as discussed below:

@ The permanent LAY list prepared for selection of beneficiaries was not in
accordance with the seniority of BPL list.
{Paragraph 2.2.8.1(b)}
© State share was released with delay ranging between two to 744 days.
(Paragraph 2.2.8.3{a})
# JAY funds in Janpad Panchayats (175) were not kept in separate bank
accounts and the interest generated could not be properly assessed. Further,
interest generated from [AY funds was not utilised for scheme activities.
{Paragraph 2.2.8.3(b) and 2.2.8.3(c)}
@ There was diversion of ¢ 104.70 lakh from the [AY funds in disregard to the
scheme guidelines.
{Paragraph 2.2.8.3()}
e@ Lack of initiation for convergence with other schemes for providing basic
facilities deprived the beneficiaries from availing the facilities of a complete
functional dwelling unit.
{Paragraph 2.2.8.4(a), 2.2.8.4(b), 2.2.8.4(c) and 2.2.8.4(e)}
@ There had been lack of training and initiation for creating awareness among
the IAY beneficiaries for Differential Rate of Interest scheme, Credit-cum-
Subsidy scheme and use of cost effective, environment friendly technologies.
{Paragraph 2.2.8.4(e), 2.2.8.5(a) and 2.2.8.5(d)}
@ Records pertaining to IAY waitlist, database detailing year wise list of
beneficiaries, date of release of installment, status of work, inventory of
houses etc. were not properly maintained by Janpad Panchayats.
{Paragraph 2.2.8.5(b)}
@ Awaas Soft, a web enabled software of IAY for facilitating e-Govemance of
the scheme through Management Information System (MIS) was not updated
leading to differences in the data between Monthly Progress Reports and MIS.
{Paragraph 2.2.8.5())}

Audit Report on General, Social and Economic (Non-PSUs) Sectors for the year ended 31 March 2013

Introduction

Indira Awaas Yojana (99), a flagship scheme of the Ministry of Rural
Development (MoRD), Government of India (Gol) was launched during 1985-86
as a sub scheme of Rural Landless Employment Guarantee Programme (RLEGP)
to provide houses to the poor in the rural areas. Since the launching of Jawahar
Rozgar Yojana (JRY) in April 1989, LAY was continued as a sub scheme of JRY
and implemented as an independent scheme from January 1996 onwards. IAY
aims at providing financial assistance for construction/upgradation of dwelling
units to the poor families of the Scheduled Castes (SC), Scheduled Tribes (ST),
free bonded labourers, minorities and non-SC/ST Below Poverty Line (BPL) rural
households, widows and next-of-kin of defence personnel/paramilitary forces
killed in action residing in rural areas (irrespective of their income criteria), ex-
servicemen and retired members of paramilitary forces fulfilling the other
conditions. Convergence for [AY houses was to be provided with Total Sanitation
Campaign (TSC), Rajiv Gandhi Gramin Vidyutikaran Yojana (RGVY) and
National Rural Drinking Water Supply Scheme (NRWSS). The Homestead
Scheme was launched for providing house site to rural landless BPL households.
The Department of Panchayat and Rural Development, Government of
Chhattisgarh (Department) is responsible for the implementation of the scheme in
the State.

2.2.2 Organisational Set-up

The Additional Chief Secretary (ACS) is the head of the Department at the
Government level and Development Commissioner (DC) under the ACS carries
out implementation of the scheme at the State level. The implementing agencies
at the District and Block are Chief Executing Officers (CEO) of Zilla Panchayats
(ZPs) and Janpad Panchayats (125) respectively and Panchayat Secretary at the
Gram Panchayat level. The Assistant Development Extension Officers (ADEOs)
and Secretaries of Gram Panchayat under the CEOs of JPs are the officers
responsible for monitoring the scheme at the grass root level.

Organisational Setup of Panchayat and Rural Development Department,

Chapter 2:Performance Audit

The objectives of the Performance Audit were to assess whether:

the selection of beneficiaries was in accordance with the scheme
guidelines;

the physical performance of units constructed and upgraded was as
planned and targeted and that the construction corresponded to the quality
and financial parameters set out in the scheme guidelines;

the allocation and release of funds under [AY were made in an adequate
and timely manner and that these were utilized economically and
efficiently in accordance with the scheme provisions;

the convergence of the LAY activities with other programmes as envisaged
was effectively achieved and availability of a complete functional
dwelling unit was ensured; and

the mechanism in place for monitoring and evaluation of the outcomes of
the programme were adequate and effective.

2.2.4 Audit Criteri

The audit criteria has been derived from the following sources:

Guidelines of Indira Awas Yojana issued by the Ministry of Rural
Development, Department of Rural Development, Gol;
Circulars/instructions issued by the Department of Rural Development,
Gol;

Circulars/instructions issued by the Department of Panchayat and Rural
Development, Financial code and Treasury code of Government of
Chhattisgarh; and

Progress Reports of the scheme at various levels, Audit Reports of the
Chartered Accountants and other records relevant to the scheme.

id audit methodology

The Performance Audit was conducted for the period from 2008-09 to 2012-13
involving test check of records at Office of the Development Commissioner (DC),
Raipur at the State level, ZPs at the district level and JPs at the block level. Besides,
examination of records in six ZPs and 15 Janpad Panchayats’ (JPs) of six’ selected
districts, beneficiary survey was also conducted in 214 villages of 149 Gram
Panchayats (GPs) under the selected JPs.

The Entry Conference was held with ACS on 13 May 2013 to discuss the audit
objectives and audit criteria adopted for the Performance Audit. The field audit for
the Performance Audit was conducted between May 2013 and July 2013. The Exit

Conference to discuss the audit observations was held with ACS on 16 January

2.2.6 Funding Pattern

Indira Awaas Yojana, a Centrally Sponsored Scheme is funded on cost-sharing
basis between the Government of India and the State Governments in the ratio of
75:25. The Indira Awaas Yojana funds are operated by the ZPs at the district level.
Central assistance is released by MoRD every year to the ZPs, in two installments.
The first installment equivalent to 50 per cent of the total allocation for a particular
district is released in the beginning of the financial year. The second installment 15
released by MoRD on the request of the ZPs subject to utilization of the funds
available and fulfillment of other conditions laid down in the scheme guidelines.

2.2.7 Planning

According to para 4.2(viii) of guidelines, Annual Plan should be approved by the
ZP or the Governing Body of the District Rural Development Authority (DRDA)
as the case may be, for release of second installment.

During the audit of selected ZPs, it was observed that Annual Plan was not
prepared. However, department extended the benefit of allotment of [AY houses to
the targeted beneficiaries on the basis of targets received from Gol.

During the exit conference (January 2014), the Additional Chief Secretary (ACS),
while accepting the facts, stated that the targets were provided by Government of
India (Gol), the State Government is not in a position to decide the targets.
However, efforts would be initiated to ensure the preparation of Annual Plan.

Reply confirms that the Annual Plan was not being prepared by the ZPs or DRDAs
which was an essential condition for release of second installment.

units for SC/ST BPL households, a maximum 40 per cent for non SC/ST BPL
tural households and three percent of the above categories is earmarked for
physically and mentally challenged persons.

beneficiaries.

2.2.8.1(b) Improper preparation of IAY waitlist

According to para 2.1 of the IAY guidelines, the District Panchayat/Zilla
Panchayat/District Rural Development Agencies (DRDAs) on the basis of
allocations made and targets fixed shall decide the number of houses to be
constructed/upgraded Panchayat-wise under [AY, during a particular financial
year. The same shall be intimated to the Gram Panchayat concerned. Thereafter,
the beneficiaries, restricting to this number, will be selected from the Permanent
1609 waitlist prepared on the basis of BPL list in order of seniority in the list. The
Gram Panchayats may draw out the shelterless families from the BPL List strictly
in the order of ranking in the list. Once the lists are prepared, they need to be
approved by the Gram Sabha (GS). Selection by the GS is final and no approval of
any higher body is required. Zilla Parishads and Block Development Offices
should, however, be sent a list of selected beneficiaries for their information.

Test check of BPL list and [AY waitlist at selected JPs and GPs, revealed
instances where seniority of BPL list was not maintained while preparing the 10५
waitlist as discussed in the following paragraphs.

(0) During the test check of records in JP Dharsiwa (Raipur district) and GP
Ajgarbahar of JP Korba (Korba district), it was noticed that the order of
beneficiaries in the [AY waitlist was not in accordance with the seniority of BPL
list. Instances were noticed where certain families having higher seniority in the
BPL list were allotted a lower seniority in the [AY waitlist. Since the financial
assistance under the scheme was provided in the order of seniority in the TAY
waitlist, faulty preparation of [AY waitlist had led to extension of assistance to
beneficiaries with lower seniority in the BPL list (2007-08 to 2010-11) as shown
in Appendix 2.2.1.

On this being pointed out, CEO JP Dharsiwa stated that the preparation and
changes of the IAY waitlist was as per the recommendation of Gram Sabha.
However, CEO JP Korba accepted the audit observations.

During the exit conference (January 2014), the ACS stated that the IAY waitlist in
the State of Chhattisgarh is prepared on the basis of need and prevailing financial
conditions of the beneficiaries. It was also stated that the list was approved by the
Gram Sabhas. However, it was assured that the matter would be reviewed and
required instructions would be issued.

Reply of ACS is not in consonance with IAY guidelines, which prescribes for
preparation of IAY waitlist strictly in the order of the BPL list.

(ii) Further, it was also noticed that in the year 2009-10, two beneficiaries of
GP, Barpali of JP Korba in Korba district were provided assistance whose names
had not figured in the BPL list.

On this being pointed out, Department informed that these beneficiaries were
relatives of the actual beneficiaries listed in the BPL list

During the exit conference (January 2014), the ACS stated that the matter would
be examined.

The reply confirms allotment of [AY houses in the name of the relatives of BPL.
household, which is not as per the provisions of the guidelines.

46


Chapter 2:Performance Audit

(iii) ~~ In JP Chhura of Raipur district, it was observed that JP was following the
practice of preparation of a new waitlist every year. The preparation of new
waitlist without exhausting the previous waitlist creates the possibility of
inclusion of non-cligible benefi sand exclusion of cligible beneficiaries in
the waitlist.

During the test check of records in one selected GP Karkara of JP Chhura, it was
observed that waitlist for the year 2012-13 included only 30 beneficiaries whereas
the previous waitlist (2011-12) included the names of 223 beneficiaries. The
names of 193 bene’ ies were excluded in the new waitlist. It was seen from
the records that GP Karkara recommended for inclusion of 193 beneficiaries in
IAY waitlist as they were eligible beneficiaries. However, no further action was
available in the records of JP. Thus, improper preparation of LAY waitlist led to
exclusion of 193 eligible beneficiaries.

On this being pointed out, CEO, JP, Chhura stated that necessary investigation
would be made in the matter and intimated to audit.
During the exit conference (January 2014), the ACS stated that according to

verification of IAY beneficiaries by JP, it was found that 193 bene
ineligible for assistance under TAY.

Reply is not acceptable as no such record of verification was produced to audit by
JP during audit and GP had also again recommended (April 2013) inclusion of
these 193 beneficiaries in the IAY waitlist.

(iv) During scrutiny of records at JP Mainpaat in Sarguja district, it was noticed
that IAY waitlist prepared during the period 2006-07 was exhausted in 2009-10
and a fresh IAY waitlist was prepared during 2009-10. During this period, another
JAY list was also prepared for providing assistance to BPL population affected by
the Left Wing Extremism.

Test check of records revealed that in GP Narmadapur under JP Mainpaat, there
were 22 BPL families (Appendix 2.2.2) who were not included in any of the [AY
waitlist. Despite this, they were sanctioned assistance under IAY in the year
2009-10. This clearly highlights the improper preparation of IAY waitlist.

On this being pointed out, Department stated that the above beneficiaries were left
out during the preparation of [AY waitlist. However, Gram Sabha approved the
nomination of these beneficiaries and thus were provided assistance.

The fact remains that Gram Sabha approved the name of the beneficiaries
separately who were not included in the [AY waitlist without specifying any
reason for such inclusion.

During the exit conference (January 2014), the ACS stated that the directions
would be issued for preparation of IAY waitlist as per the guidelines.

cial Performance

State Government is responsible for releasing the matching State share as well as
for implementation of the scheme.

2.2.8.2 (b) (ii) Sanction of assistance to male members instead of female
members of the beneficiary family

According to para 2.4 of the IAY guidelines, allotment of dwelling units should
be in the name of female member of the beneficiary household. Alternatively, it
can be allotted in the name of both husband and wife. However, if there is no
eligible female member in the family available/alive, house can also be allotted to
the male member of a deserving BPL family.

share to the ZPs/DRDAs within one month after the release of Central assistance
and copy of the same should be endorsed to MoRD. Further, as per para 4.8 ibid,
the interest amount accrued on the deposits of the IAY funds shall be treated as
part of the [AY resources.

2.2.8.3(€) Non-maintenance of separate Bank Account for [AY

According to para 4.7 of [AY guidelines, the IAY funds (Central share as well as
State share) shall be kept in a Nationalized/scheduled or cooperative bank or a
Post Office in an exclusive and separate savings bank account. Further, para 4.8
provides that the interest earned on deposits of the [AY funds shall be treated as
part of the [AY resources.

No separate bank accounts for [AY funds were opened in eight’ out of the
selected 15 JPs. As a result, interest earned from the [AY funds could not be
bifurcated and thus could not be used for [AY purposes. Further, prompt
accountal of the scheme funds could have been helpful for reconciliation of funds
received with the sanction orders from Gol as mentioned in paragraph 2.2.8.3(a)
and also in the subsequent paragraph.

e Scrutiny of records of ZP Korba, revealed that the Central share of [AY
funds from Gol was received through Real Time Gross Settlement (RTGS) in the
account maintained with State Bank of India (SBI) Korba. This Current Account
was opened in favour of CEO, ZP Korba and funds of other schemes were also
received in this bank account. After receipt of IAY funds from Gol in this
account, the same was transferred to another bank (Central Bank of India/Union
Bank of India) which was opened separately for 189, Scrutiny of records
revealed that Gol funds ranging from % 4.51 lakh to % 2.18 crore were transferred.
to the IAY account after delay of two to 34 days (Appendix 2.2.5). The parking of

schemes. The delay in transfer was due to delay in receipt of sanction orders only

observation, stated that instructions would be issued to all the ZPs/JPs to open
